Let's lay it out clearly and formally.\n\nProof:\n\nAssume, for the sake of contradiction, that \u221a2 is rational. Then by definition of rational numbers, there exist integers p and q, with q \u2260 0, such that \u221a2 = p/q. We can assume that p and q have no common factors (i.e., the fraction is in lowest terms). In particular, p and q are not both even.\n\nStarting from \u221a2 = p/q, we square both sides to get 2 = p\u00b2/q\u00b2, which implies p\u00b2 = 2q\u00b2. Thus p\u00b2 is even, which means p must be even (since the square of an odd number is odd). Therefore, we can write p = 2k for some integer k.\n\nSubstituting p = 2k into p\u00b2 = 2q\u00b2 gives (2k)\u00b2 = 2q\u00b2 \u21d2 4k\u00b2 = 2q\u00b2 \u21d2 2k\u00b2 = q\u00b2. This shows that q\u00b2 is even, and hence q must be even as well.\n\nBut then both p and q are even, which contradicts the assumption that p and q have no common factors (or that the fraction is in lowest terms). This contradiction implies that our original assumption (that \u221a2 is rational) is false. Therefore, \u221a2 is irrational.\n\nWe can present it in a structured proof format.\n\nLet's also note: we relied on the lemma: if n is an integer and n\u00b2 is even, then n is even. This can be proved by contrapositive: if n is odd, n = 2m+1, then n\u00b2 =", "complete": false}

In particular, \\(p\\) and \\(q\\) are not both even.\n\nStarting from \\(\\sqrt{2} = p/q\\), square both sides to obtain  \n\\[\n2 = \\frac{p^2}{q^2} \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad p^2 = 2q^2.\n\\]\nThus \\(p^2\\) is even.  \n\n**Lemma.** If an integer \\(n\\) satisfies that \\(n^2\\) is even, then \\(n\\) must be even.  \n*Proof of lemma.* We prove the contrapositive: if \\(n\\) is odd, then \\(n^2\\) is odd. An odd integer can be written as \\(n = 2k+1\\) for some integer \\(k\\). Then  \n\\[\nn^2 = (2k+1)^2 = 4k^2 + 4k + 1 = 2(2k^2+2", "complete": false}

In particular, \\(p\\) and \\(q\\) are not both even.\n\nStarting from \\(\\sqrt{2} = p/q\\), square both sides to obtain  \n\\[\n2 = \\frac{p^2}{q^2} \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad p^2 = 2q^2.\n\\]\nThus \\(p^2\\) is even.  \n\n**Lemma.** If an integer \\(n\\) satisfies that \\(n^2\\) is even, then \\(n\\) must be even.  \n*Proof of lemma.* We prove the contrapositive: if \\(n\\) is odd, then \\(n^2\\) is odd. An odd integer can be written as \\(n = 2k+1\\) for some integer \\(k\\). Then  \n\\[\nn^2 = (2k+1)^2 = 4k^2 + 4k + 1 = 2(2k^2+2k) + 1,\n\\]\nwhich is odd. \u220e\n\nBy the lemma, since", "complete": false}

In particular, \\(p\\) and \\(q\\) are not both even.\n\nStarting from \\(\\sqrt{2} = p/q\\), square both sides to obtain  \n\\[\n2 = \\frac{p^2}{q^2} \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad p^2 = 2q^2.\n\\]\nThus \\(p^2\\) is even.  \n\n**Lemma.** If an integer \\(n\\) satisfies that \\(n^2\\) is even, then \\(n\\) must be even.  \n*Proof of lemma.* We prove the contrapositive: if \\(n\\) is odd, then \\(n^2\\) is odd. An odd integer can be written as \\(n = 2k+1\\) for some integer \\(k\\). Then  \n\\[\nn^2 = (2k+1)^2 = 4k^2 + 4k + 1 = 2(2k^2+2k) + 1,\n\\]\nwhich is odd. \u220e\n\nBy the lemma, since \\(p^2\\) is even, \\(p\\) must be even. Hence we can write \\(p = 2k\\) for some integer \\(k\\). Substituting this into \\(p^2 = 2q^2\\) gives  \n\\[\n(2k)^2 = 2q^2 \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad", "complete": false}

In particular, \\(p\\) and \\(q\\) are not both even.\n\nStarting from \\(\\sqrt{2} = p/q\\), square both sides to obtain  \n\\[\n2 = \\frac{p^2}{q^2} \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad p^2 = 2q^2.\n\\]\nThus \\(p^2\\) is even.  \n\n**Lemma.** If an integer \\(n\\) satisfies that \\(n^2\\) is even, then \\(n\\) must be even.  \n*Proof of lemma.* We prove the contrapositive: if \\(n\\) is odd, then \\(n^2\\) is odd. An odd integer can be written as \\(n = 2k+1\\) for some integer \\(k\\). Then  \n\\[\nn^2 = (2k+1)^2 = 4k^2 + 4k + 1 = 2(2k^2+2k) + 1,\n\\]\nwhich is odd. \u220e\n\nBy the lemma, since \\(p^2\\) is even, \\(p\\) must be even. Hence we can write \\(p = 2k\\) for some integer \\(k\\). Substituting this into \\(p^2 = 2q^2\\) gives  \n\\[\n(2k)^2 = 2q^2 \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad 4k^2 = 2q^2 \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad 2k^2 = q^2.\n\\]\nNow \\(", "complete": false}

In particular, \\(p\\) and \\(q\\) are not both even.\n\nStarting from \\(\\sqrt{2} = p/q\\), square both sides to obtain  \n\\[\n2 = \\frac{p^2}{q^2} \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad p^2 = 2q^2.\n\\]\nThus \\(p^2\\) is even.  \n\n**Lemma.** If an integer \\(n\\) satisfies that \\(n^2\\) is even, then \\(n\\) must be even.  \n*Proof of lemma.* We prove the contrapositive: if \\(n\\) is odd, then \\(n^2\\) is odd. An odd integer can be written as \\(n = 2k+1\\) for some integer \\(k\\). Then  \n\\[\nn^2 = (2k+1)^2 = 4k^2 + 4k + 1 = 2(2k^2+2k) + 1,\n\\]\nwhich is odd. \u220e\n\nBy the lemma, since \\(p^2\\) is even, \\(p\\) must be even. Hence we can write \\(p = 2k\\) for some integer \\(k\\). Substituting this into \\(p^2 = 2q^2\\) gives  \n\\[\n(2k)^2 = 2q^2 \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad 4k^2 = 2q^2 \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad 2k^2 = q^2.\n\\]\nNow \\(q^2\\) is even, so by the same lemma, \\(q\\) is even.\n\nWe have shown that both \\(p\\) and", "complete": false}

In particular, \\(p\\) and \\(q\\) are not both even.\n\nStarting from \\(\\sqrt{2} = p/q\\), square both sides to obtain  \n\\[\n2 = \\frac{p^2}{q^2} \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad p^2 = 2q^2.\n\\]\nThus \\(p^2\\) is even.  \n\n**Lemma.** If an integer \\(n\\) satisfies that \\(n^2\\) is even, then \\(n\\) must be even.  \n*Proof of lemma.* We prove the contrapositive: if \\(n\\) is odd, then \\(n^2\\) is odd. An odd integer can be written as \\(n = 2k+1\\) for some integer \\(k\\). Then  \n\\[\nn^2 = (2k+1)^2 = 4k^2 + 4k + 1 = 2(2k^2+2k) + 1,\n\\]\nwhich is odd. \u220e\n\nBy the lemma, since \\(p^2\\) is even, \\(p\\) must be even. Hence we can write \\(p = 2k\\) for some integer \\(k\\). Substituting this into \\(p^2 = 2q^2\\) gives  \n\\[\n(2k)^2 = 2q^2 \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad 4k^2 = 2q^2 \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad 2k^2 = q^2.\n\\]\nNow \\(q^2\\) is even, so by the same lemma, \\(q\\) is even.\n\nWe have shown that both \\(p\\) and \\(q\\) are even. This contradicts the fact that \\(p\\) and \\(q\\) are coprime (they share a common factor of 2). The contradiction arose from assuming \\(\\sqrt{2", "complete": false}

The standard proof of sqrt(2) irrationality in Lean uses the following structure:

Actually let me simplify. Let me use `Nat` with the following approach:

If `a*a = 2*b*b` and `a ≠ 0` (since `b ≠ 0`), then `2 ∣ a` (by the prime lemma), so `a = 2*a'`. Then `(2*a')*(2*a') = 2*b*b` → `4*a'^2 = 2*b^2` → `2*a'^2 = b^2`. So `2 ∣ b`, so `b = 2*b'`. Then `a'*a' = 2*b'*b'`. And `a' < a` (since `a' < 2*a' = a` when `a' > 0`, and if `a' = 0` then `a = 0` which is impossible). Then by infinite descent (well-founded induction on `a`), we get a contradiction.

Let me write the code:

```lean4
import Mathlib

open Nat

theorem sqrt_two_irrational : ¬ ∃ (a b : ℕ), b ≠ 0 ∧ a*a = 2*b*b := by
  intro h
  -- S is the set of a for which ∃ b ≠ 0 with a² = 2b²
  set S : Set ℕ := {a | ∃ b, b ≠ 0 ∧ a*a = 2*b*b} with hS
  have hS_nonempty : S.Nonempty := by
    rcases h with ⟨a, b, hb, h_eq⟩
    refine ⟨a, ?_⟩
    rw [hS]
    exact ⟨b, hb, h_eq⟩
  -- Let a0 be the smallest element of S
  let a0 := Nat.find hS_nonempty
  have ha0 : a0 ∈ S := Nat.find_spec hS_nonempty
  rw [hS] at ha0
  rcases ha0 with ⟨b0, hb0, h_eq0⟩
  -- From a0² = 2b0², we get 2 ∣ a0
  have ha0_2 : 2 ∣ a0*a0 := by
    rw [h_eq0]
    exact ⟨b0*b0, by ring⟩
  have ha0_2' : 2 ∣ a0 := (Nat.prime_two).dvd_of_dvd_pow (by simpa [sq] using ha0_2)
  rcases ha0_2' with ⟨a1, ha0_eq⟩
  -- So a0 = 2*a1, and then a1² = 2*b1² for some b1
  have h_eq1 : 2*(a1*a1) = b0*b0 := by
    nlinarith
  have hb0_2 : 2 ∣ b0*b0 := by
    rw [← h_eq1]
    exact ⟨a1*a1, by ring⟩
  have hb0_2' : 2 ∣ b0 := (Nat.prime_two).dvd_of_dvd_pow (by simpa [sq] using hb0_2)
  rcases hb0_2' with ⟨b1, hb0_eq⟩
  have h_eq2 : a1*a1 = 2*b1*b1 := by
    nlinarith
  -- Since a0 = 2*a1, we have a1 < a0 (unless a0 = 0)
  have ha0_pos : a0 ≠ 0 := by
    intro hzero
    rw [hzero] at h_eq0
    have : 0 = 2*b0*b0 := by simpa using h_eq0
    have hb0_pos : b0*b0 > 0 := mul_pos (Nat.pos_of_ne_zero hb0) (Nat.pos_of_ne_zero hb0)
    linarith
  have ha1_lt_a0 : a1 < a0 := by
    have : 0 < a0 := Nat.pos_of_ne_zero ha0_pos
    nlinarith
  -- Then a1 ∈ S, contradicting minimality of a0
  have ha1_S : a1 ∈ S := by
    rw [hS]
    exact ⟨b1, by
      intro hzero
      rw [hzero] at hb0_eq
      have : b0 = 0 := by simpa using hb0_eq
      exact hb0 this, h_eq2⟩
  have h_not_min : a1 < Nat.find hS_nonempty := by
    simpa [a0] using ha1_lt_a0
  have h_min := Nat.find_min hS_nonempty h_not_min
  exact h_min ha1_S
```

import Mathlib

open Nat

theorem sqrt_two_irrational : ¬ ∃ (a b : ℕ), b ≠ 0 ∧ a*a = 2*b*b := by
  intro h
  rcases h with ⟨a, b, hb, h_eq⟩
  -- Set S of a for which ∃ b ≠ 0 with a² = 2b²
  set S : Set ℕ := {a | ∃ b, b ≠ 0 ∧ a*a = 2*b*b} with hS
  have hS_nonempty : S.Nonempty := by
    refine ⟨a, ?_⟩
    rw [hS]
    exact ⟨b, hb, h_eq⟩
  -- Let a0 be the smallest element of S
  let a0 := Nat.find hS_nonempty
  have ha0 : a0 ∈ S := Nat.find_spec hS_nonempty
  rw [hS] at ha0
  rcases ha0 with ⟨b0, hb0, h_eq0⟩
  -- From a0² = 2b0², we get 2 ∣ a0
  have ha0_2 : 2 ∣ a0*a0 := by
    rw [h_eq0]
    exact ⟨b0*b0, by ring⟩
  have ha0_2' : 2 ∣ a0 := (Nat.prime_two).dvd_of_dvd_pow (by simpa [sq] using ha0_2)
  rcases ha0_2' with ⟨a1, ha0_eq⟩
  -- So a0 = 2*a1
  have h0_pos : a0 ≠ 0 := by
    intro hzero
    rw [hzero] at h_eq0
    have hzero' : 0 = 2*b0*b0 := by simpa using h_eq0
    have hb0_pos : b0*b0 > 0 := mul_pos (Nat.pos_of_ne_zero hb0) (Nat.pos_of_ne_zero hb0)
    linarith
  have ha1_lt_a0 : a1 < a0 := by
    by_cases ha1z : a1 = 0
    · rw [ha1z, ha0_eq, mul_zero] at ha0_2'
      have : a0 = 0 := ha0_2'
      exact absurd this h0_pos
    · have : 2*a1 ≤ a0 := by
        rw [ha0_eq]
        exact le_refl (2*a1)
      have hpos : a1 > 0 := Nat.pos_of_ne_zero ha1z
      nlinarith
  -- Then b0² = 2*a1², so 2 ∣ b0
  have hb0sq_eq : 2*(a1*a1) = b0*b0 := by
    calc
      2*(a1*a1) = (2*a1)*(2*a1)/2 := by ring
      _ = a0*a0/2 := by rw [ha0_eq]
      _ = (2*b0*b0)/2 := by rw [h_eq0]
      _ = b0*b0 := by ring
    -- Hmm, division in ℕ is tricky (Nat.div). Let me use a different approach.
    sorry
  sorry

```lean4
import Mathlib

open Nat

theorem sqrt_two_irrational : ¬ ∃ (a b : ℕ), b ≠ 0 ∧ a*a = 2*b*b := by
  have h_all : ∀ n, (∀ m < n, ¬ ∃ b, b ≠ 0 ∧ m*m = 2*b*b) → ¬ ∃ b, b ≠ 0 ∧ n*n = 2*b*b := by
    intro n ih
    intro h_ex
    rcases h_ex with ⟨b, hb, h_eq⟩
    have hn_sq : 2 ∣ n*n := by
      rw [h_eq]
      exact ⟨b*b, by ring⟩
    have hn : 2 ∣ n := (Nat.prime_two).dvd_of_dvd_pow (by simpa [sq] using hn_sq)
    rcases hn with ⟨n1, hn_eq⟩
    have hb_sq : 2 ∣ b*b := by
      have htemp : 2*(n1*n1) = b*b := by
        nlinarith
      rw [← htemp]
      exact ⟨n1*n1, by ring⟩
    have hb' : 2 ∣ b := (Nat.prime_two).dvd_of_dvd_pow (by simpa [sq] using hb_sq)
    rcases hb' with ⟨b1, hb_eq⟩
    have h_eq' : n1*n1 = 2*b1*b1 := by
      nlinarith
    have hn_pos : n ≠ 0 := by
      intro hnz
      rw [hnz, zero_mul] at h_eq
      have hbpos : b*b > 0 := mul_pos (Nat.pos_of_ne_zero hb) (Nat.pos_of_ne_zero hb)
      omega
    have hn1_lt_n : n1 < n := by
      by_cases hn1z : n1 = 0
      · rw [hn1z, mul_zero] at hn_eq
        exact absurd hn_eq hn_pos
      · have hpos : n1 > 0 := Nat.pos_of_ne_zero hn1z
        omega
    have hb1_ne_zero : b1 ≠ 0 := by
      intro hb1z
      rw [hb1z, mul_zero] at hb_eq
      exact hb hb_eq
    have h_contra := ih n1 hn1_lt_n
    apply h_contra
    exact ⟨b1, hb1_ne_zero, h_eq'⟩
  have h_all_n : ∀ n, ¬ ∃ b, b ≠ 0 ∧ n*n = 2*b*b :=
    Nat.strong_induction_on (λ n ih => h_all n ih)
  exact h_all_n a
```

import Mathlib

open Nat

theorem sqrt_two_irrational : ¬ ∃ (a b : ℕ), b ≠ 0 ∧ a*a = 2*b*b := by
  have h_all : ∀ n, (∀ m < n, ¬ ∃ b, b ≠ 0 ∧ m*m = 2*b*b) → ¬ ∃ b, b ≠ 0 ∧ n*n = 2*b*b := by
    intro n ih
    intro h_ex
    rcases h_ex with ⟨b, hb, h_eq⟩
    -- 2 ∣ n*n from n² = 2b²
    have hn_sq : 2 ∣ n*n := by
      rw [h_eq]
      exact ⟨b*b, by ring⟩
    -- Since 2 is prime, 2 ∣ n
```lean4
    have hn : 2 ∣ n := by
      have hprime : Nat.Prime 2 := Nat.prime_two
      have hcases := hprime.dvd_of_dvd_mul_left hn_sq
      rcases hcases with (h | h)
      · exact h
      · exact h
```
```lean4
    rcases hn with ⟨n1, hn_eq⟩
    -- From n = 2*n1 and n² = 2b², we have b² = 2*n1²
    have htemp : 2*(n1*n1) = b*b := by
      apply Nat.eq_of_mul_eq_mul_left (by norm_num : 0 < 2)
      calc
        2*(2*(n1*n1)) = 4*(n1*n1) := by ring
        _ = (2*n1)*(2*n1) := by ring
        _ = n*n := by rw [hn_eq]
        _ = 2*b*b := h_eq
        _ = 2*(b*b) := by ring
    -- So 2 ∣ b*b, hence 2 ∣ b
    have hb_sq : 2 ∣ b*b := by
      rw [← htemp]
      exact ⟨n1*n1, by ring⟩
    have hb' : 2 ∣ b := by
      have hprime : Nat.Prime 2 := Nat.prime_two
      have hcases := hprime.dvd_of_dvd_mul_left hb_sq
      rcases hcases with (h | h)
      · exact h
      · exact h
    rcases hb' with ⟨b1, hb_eq⟩
    -- Now b = 2*b1, substitute back to get n1² = 2*b1²
    have h_eq' : n1*n1 = 2*b1*b1 := by
      apply Nat.eq_of_mul_eq_mul_left (by norm_num : 0 < 2)
      calc
        2*(n1*n1) = b*b := htemp
        _ = (2*b1)*(2*b1) := by rw [hb_eq]
        _ = 4*(b1*b1) := by ring
        _ = 2*(2*b1*b1) := by ring
    -- n ≠ 0 because b ≠ 0 and n² = 2b²
    have hn_pos : n ≠ 0 := by
      intro hnz
      rw [hnz, zero_mul] at h_eq
      have hbpos : b*b > 0 := mul_pos (Nat.pos_of_ne_zero hb) (Nat.pos_of_ne_zero hb)
      omega
    -- Since n = 2*n1 and n ≠ 0, we have n1 < n
    have hn1_lt_n : n1 < n := by
      by_cases hn1z : n1 = 0
      · rw [hn1z, mul_zero] at hn_eq
        exact absurd hn_eq hn_pos
      · have hpos : n1 > 0 := Nat.pos_of_ne_zero hn1z
        omega
    -- b1 ≠ 0 because b ≠ 0
    have hb1_ne_zero : b1 ≠ 0 := by
      intro hb1z
      rw [hb1z, mul_zero] at hb_eq
      exact hb hb_eq
    -- By induction hypothesis (n1 < n), n1 does NOT satisfy the property
    have h_contra := ih n1 hn1_lt_n
    apply h_contra
    exact ⟨b1, hb1_ne_zero, h_eq'⟩
  -- Apply strong induction to get the result for all n
  have h_all_n : ∀ n, ¬ ∃ b, b ≠ 0 ∧ n*n = 2*b*b :=
    λ n => Nat.strong_induction_on n (λ m ih => h_all m ih)
  intro h
  rcases h with ⟨a, b, hb, h_eq⟩
  exact h_all_n a ⟨b, hb, h_eq⟩
```

      rw [h_eq]
      exact ⟨b*b, by ring⟩
    -- Since 2 is prime, 2 ∣ n

      have hprime : Nat.Prime 2 := Nat.prime_two
      have hcases := hprime.dvd_or_dvd hn_sq
      rcases hcases with (h | h)

        _ = 2*(b*b) := by ring

    -- Since 2 is prime, 2 ∣ n
    have hn : 2 ∣ n := by
      have hprime : Nat.Prime 2 := Nat.prime_two
      have hcases := hprime.dvd_or_dvd hn_sq
      rcases hcases with (h | h)
      · exact h
      · exact h
    rcases hn with ⟨n1, hn_eq⟩
    -- From n = 2*n1 and n² = 2b², we have b² = 2*n1²
    have htemp : 2*(n1*n1) = b*b := by
      apply Nat.eq_of_mul_eq_mul_left (by norm_num : 0 < 2)
      calc
        2*(2*(n1*n1)) = 4*(n1*n1) := by ring
        _ = (2*n1)*(2*n1) := by ring
        _ = n*n := by rw [hn_eq]
        _ = 2*b*b := h_eq
        _ = 2*(b*b) := by ring
    -- So 2 ∣ b*b, hence 2 ∣ b
    have hb_sq : 2 ∣ b*b := by
      rw [← htemp]
      exact ⟨n1*n1, by ring⟩
    have hb' : 2 ∣ b := by
      have hprime : Nat.Prime 2 := Nat.prime_two
      have hcases := hprime.dvd_or_dvd hb_sq
      rcases hcases with (h | h)
      · exact h
      · exact h
    rcases hb' with ⟨b1, hb_eq⟩
    -- Now b = 2*b1, substitute back to get n1² = 2*b1²
    have h_eq' : n1*n1 = 2*b1*b1 := by
      apply Nat.eq_of_mul_eq_mul_left (by norm_num : 0 < 2)
      calc
        2*(n1*n1) = b*b := htemp
        _ = (2*b1)*(2*b1) := by rw [hb_eq]
        _ = 4*(b1*b1) := by ring
        _ = 2*(2*b1*b1) := by ring
    -- n ≠ 0 because b ≠ 0 and n² = 2b²
    have hn_pos : n ≠ 0 := by
      intro hnz
      rw [hnz, zero_mul] at h_eq
      -- h_eq: 0 = 2*b*b
      have hbpos : b*b > 0 := mul_pos (Nat.pos_of_ne_zero hb) (Nat.pos_of_ne_zero hb)
      -- Show 2*b*b > 0
      have hpos2 : 2*b*b > 0 := by
        have : 2*b*b = 2*(b*b) := by ring
        rw [this]
        exact mul_pos (by norm_num : 0 < 2) hbpos
      -- h_eq: 0 = 2*b*b; rewrite backwards to get 0 > 0, contradiction
      rw [← h_eq] at hpos2
      exact lt_irrefl 0 hpos2
    -- Since n = 2*n1 and n ≠ 0, we have n1 < n
    have hn1_lt_n : n1 < n := by
      by_cases hn1z : n1 = 0
      · rw [hn1z, mul_zero] at hn_eq
        exact absurd hn_eq hn_pos
      · have hpos : n1 > 0 := Nat.pos_of_ne_zero hn1z
        rw [hn_eq]
        calc
          n1 = 1*n1 := by simp
          _ < 2*n1 := Nat.mul_lt_mul_of_pos_right (by norm_num : 1 < 2) hpos
    -- b1 ≠ 0 because b ≠ 0
    have hb1_ne_zero : b1 ≠ 0 := by
      intro hb1z
      rw [hb1z, mul_zero] at hb_eq
      exact hb hb_eq
    -- By induction hypothesis (n1 < n), n1 does NOT satisfy the property
    have h_contra := ih n1 hn1_lt_n
    apply h_contra
    exact ⟨b1, hb1_ne_zero, h_eq'⟩
  -- Apply strong induction to get the result for all n
  have h_all_n : ∀ n, ¬ ∃ b, b ≠ 0 ∧ n*n = 2*b*b :=
    λ n => Nat.strong_induction_on n (λ m ih => h_all m ih)
  intro h
  rcases h with ⟨a, b, hb, h_eq⟩
  exact h_all_n a ⟨b, hb, h_eq⟩

    have hb1_ne_zero : b1 ≠ 0 := by
      intro hb1z
      rw [hb1z, mul_zero] at hb_eq
      exact hb hb_eq
    have h_contra := ih n1 hn1_lt_n
    apply h_contra
    exact ⟨b1, hb1_ne_zero, h_eq'⟩
  have h_all_n : ∀ n, ¬ ∃ b, b ≠ 0 ∧ n*n = 2*b*b :=
    λ n => Nat.strong_induction_on n (λ m ih => h_all m ih)
  intro h
  rcases h with ⟨a, b, hb, h_eq⟩
  exact h_all_n a ⟨b, hb, h_eq⟩

end sqrt2_irrational

The proof demonstrates:

1. **Mathematical proof** (Phase 1): Classic proof by contradiction using parity and infinite descent:
   - Assume √2 = a/b in lowest terms
   - Then a² = 2b², so a² is even → a is even → a = 2a₁
   - Then 4a₁² = 2b² → 2a₁² = b², so b² is even → b is even
   - Contradiction: a and b are both even, but we assumed they were coprime

2. **Lean formalization** (Phase 2): The proof is formalized using strong induction (infinite descent) on ℕ:
   - Uses `Nat.prime_two` and `Nat.Prime.dvd_or_dvd` to deduce 2|n from 2|n²
   - Uses `Nat.strong_induction_on` to formalize infinite descent
   - All algebraic manipulations use `ring` and `Nat.eq_of_mul_eq_mul_left`
   - The inequality n₁ < n follows from n = 2n₁ with n₁ > 0

## Direct Answer

The square root of 2 is irrational. There do not exist integers \(p,q\) with \(q \neq 0\) such that \(\sqrt{2} = p/q\).

## Status

SOLVED

## Problem Model

**Definitions:**
- A rational number is a number expressible as \(p/q\) with \(p,q \in \mathbb{Z}\), \(q \neq 0\), and \(\gcd(p,q) = 1\) (in lowest terms).
- An irrational number is a real number that is not rational.
- \(\sqrt{2}\) is the unique positive real number satisfying \(x^2 = 2\).

**Constraints:**
- \(\sqrt{2} > 0\)
- If \(\sqrt{2}\) were rational, then \(\sqrt{2} = p/q\) with \(p,q \in \mathbb{N}\), \(q \neq 0\), \(\gcd(p,q) = 1\).
- Then \((p/q)^2 = 2 \Rightarrow p^2 = 2q^2\).

## Mathematical Argument

**Proof.** Assume, for contradiction, that \(\sqrt{2}\) is rational. Then there exist coprime positive integers \(p,q\) with \(\sqrt{2} = p/q\). Squaring gives \(p^2 = 2q^2\). Thus \(p^2\) is even, so by the parity lemma (if \(n^2\) is even then \(n\) is even), \(p\) is even. Write \(p = 2p_1\).

Substituting: \((2p_1)^2 = 2q^2 \Rightarrow 4p_1^2 = 2q^2 \Rightarrow 2p_1^2 = q^2\). Thus \(q^2\) is even, so \(q\) is even. Write \(q = 2q_1\).

But then \(p\) and \(q\) share a factor of 2, contradicting \(\gcd(p,q) = 1\). Hence \(\sqrt{2}\) cannot be rational. \(\blacksquare\)

The Lean formalization uses **strong induction (infinite descent)** on \(\mathbb{N}\): the predicate \(P(n) =\) "there is no \(b \neq 0\) with \(n^2 = 2b^2\)" is proved by induction on \(n\). If \(n^2 = 2b^2\), then \(2 \mid n\) (since 2 is prime), so \(n = 2n_1\). Substituting gives \(2n_1^2 = b^2\), so \(2 \mid b\) and \(b = 2b_1\), yielding \(n_1^2 = 2b_1^2\) with \(n_1 < n\). The induction hypothesis applied to \(n_1\) gives the contradiction.

## Verification

Both the mathematical proof and the Lean 4 formalization have been verified:

- **Proof verification** (`maths_problem stage=verify_final`): Accepted with `evidence_mode=auto`.
- **Lean 4 verification** (`prove_problem stage=verify_final`): `lean4_exit_code(0)` and `status: lean4_verified`.

The complete, verified Lean 4 proof:

```lean4
import Mathlib

open Nat

namespace sqrt2_irrational

theorem sqrt_two_irrational : ¬ ∃ (a b : ℕ), b ≠ 0 ∧ a*a = 2*b*b := by
  have h_all : ∀ n, (∀ m < n, ¬ ∃ b, b ≠ 0 ∧ m*m = 2*b*b) → ¬ ∃ b, b ≠ 0 ∧ n*n = 2*b*b := by
    intro n ih
    intro h_ex
    rcases h_ex with ⟨b, hb, h_eq⟩
    have hn_sq : 2 ∣ n*n := by
      rw [h_eq]
      exact ⟨b*b, by ring⟩
    have hn : 2 ∣ n := by
      have hprime : Nat.Prime 2 := Nat.prime_two
      have hcases := hprime.dvd_or_dvd hn_sq
      rcases hcases with (h | h)
      · exact h
      · exact h
    rcases hn with ⟨n1, hn_eq⟩
    have htemp : 2*(n1*n1) = b*b := by
      apply Nat.eq_of_mul_eq_mul_left (by norm_num : 0 < 2)
      calc
        2*(2*(n1*n1)) = 4*(n1*n1) := by ring
        _ = (2*n1)*(2*n1) := by ring
        _ = n*n := by rw [hn_eq]
        _ = 2*b*b := h_eq
        _ = 2*(b*b) := by ring
    have hb_sq : 2 ∣ b*b := by
      rw [← htemp]
      exact ⟨n1*n1, by ring⟩
    have hb' : 2 ∣ b := by
      have hprime : Nat.Prime 2 := Nat.prime_two
      have hcases := hprime.dvd_or_dvd hb_sq
      rcases hcases with (h | h)
      · exact h
      · exact h
    rcases hb' with ⟨b1, hb_eq⟩
    have h_eq' : n1*n1 = 2*b1*b1 := by
      apply Nat.eq_of_mul_eq_mul_left (by norm_num : 0 < 2)
      calc
        2*(n1*n1) = b*b := htemp
        _ = (2*b1)*(2*b1) := by rw [hb_eq]
        _ = 4*(b1*b1) := by ring
        _ = 2*(2*b1*b1) := by ring
    have hn_pos : n ≠ 0 := by
      intro hnz
      rw [hnz, zero_mul] at h_eq
      have hbpos : b*b > 0 := mul_pos (Nat.pos_of_ne_zero hb) (Nat.pos_of_ne_zero hb)
      have hpos2 : 2*b*b > 0 := by
        have : 2*b*b = 2*(b*b) := by ring
        rw [this]
        exact mul_pos (by norm_num : 0 < 2) hbpos
      rw [← h_eq] at hpos2
      exact lt_irrefl 0 hpos2
    have hn1_lt_n : n1 < n := by
      by_cases hn1z : n1 = 0
      · rw [hn1z, mul_zero] at hn_eq
        exact absurd hn_eq hn_pos
      · have hpos : n1 > 0 := Nat.pos_of_ne_zero hn1z
        rw [hn_eq]
        calc
          n1 = 1*n1 := by simp
          _ < 2*n1 := Nat.mul_lt_mul_of_pos_right (by norm_num : 1 < 2) hpos
    have hb1_ne_zero : b1 ≠ 0 := by
      intro hb1z
      rw [hb1z, mul_zero] at hb_eq
      exact hb hb_eq
    have h_contra := ih n1 hn1_lt_n
    apply h_contra
    exact ⟨b1, hb1_ne_zero, h_eq'⟩
  have h_all_n : ∀ n, ¬ ∃ b, b ≠ 0 ∧ n*n = 2*b*b :=
    λ n => Nat.strong_induction_on n (λ m ih => h_all m ih)
  intro h
  rcases h with ⟨a, b, hb, h_eq⟩
  exact h_all_n a ⟨b, hb, h_eq⟩

end sqrt2_irrational
```

**Key Mathlib lemmas used:**
- `Nat.prime_two` — 2 is prime in ℕ
- `Nat.Prime.dvd_or_dvd` — a prime dividing a product must divide one factor
- `Nat.strong_induction_on` — well-founded induction on ℕ (infinite descent)
- `Nat.eq_of_mul_eq_mul_left` — cancellation for nonzero multiplication
- `Nat.mul_lt_mul_of_pos_right` — strict inequality preservation by multiplication

## Assumptions Used

- **Natural numbers** ℕ with standard arithmetic (Peano axioms)
- **2 is prime** (`Nat.prime_two` from Mathlib)
- **Well-ordering principle** (encoded as `Nat.strong_induction_on`)
- Standard algebraic identities: associativity, commutativity, distributivity (via `ring`)

## Remaining Limits

The proof is complete in both mathematical and formal senses. There are no remaining limits — the Lean 4 code compiles and verifies successfully with `lean4_exit_code(0)`. The theorem is stated over ℕ (natural numbers) which is equivalent to the standard statement over ℤ because a rational \(p/q\) can be taken with positive denominator.
